- **[@tigunait2019]** Tigunait, P. R. *Vishoka Meditation: The Yoga of Inner Radiance*. Himalayan Institute, Honesdale PA. 2019. (r3, *unverified*, practitioner) — Seen only as a bibliography entry in Shafer (p. 222). It is the source of her seated 'adapted Vishoka meditation yoga nidra script' (p. 112).
- **[@tracol]** Tracol, H. *Pourquoi dors-tu, Seigneur ?* (r2, *unverified*, practitioner, fr) — Cited in the endnotes of Bonnasse 2017 (snippet). Shows a Gurdjieff-Work influence on Bonnasse's 'vigilance' framing of sleep. Publication details not checked.
- **[@troekes]** Trökes, A. *Yoga Nidra (mit CD)*. Gräfe und Unzer (GU). <https://www.thalia.de/shop/home/artikeldetails/A1032478155> (r2, confirmed, practitioner/popular/cultural, de, cited ×2) — Mass-market German guide by a prominent German yoga teacher; GU product page 404, bookseller listing title only
- **[@urlacher2022]** Urlacher, S. *The Book of Yoga Nidra Meditation Scripts: 30 Yoga Nidra Scripts for Deep Relaxation, Inner Peace, & Manifesting Your Joy*. Sacred Nature Press (self-published). 2023. <https://www.yinandmeditation.com/books/book-of-yoga-nidra-meditation-scripts-sagel-urlacher> (r3, confirmed, practitioner, cited ×2) — Project id urlacher2022 but first edition is 31 Oct 2023 (Amazon, Goodreads, Ex Libris, AbeBooks, Google Books agree). Paperback ISBN 9798985626629 (396 pp.), hardcover ASIN B0CP9S19JT (probably ISBN 9798985626643), Kindle ASIN B0CM6XVKRR (398 pp., Kindle Unlimited). 30 scripts, 25-45 min, Satyananda-style 8-stage skeleton with imagery-heavy journeys, plus restorative poses, mudras and mantras. No audio. No Open Library record.
- **[@urlacher2026]** Urlacher, S. *The Heart of Yoga Nidra: Teaching Yoga Nidra Through Sequencing, Language, and Guided Meditation for Deep Relaxation and Sacred Rest*. Sacred Nature Press (self-published). 2026. <https://www.yinandmeditation.com/books/heart-of-yoga-nidra-book-sagel-urlacher> (r3, read, practitioner, cited ×1) — Companion teaching manual (selected as urlacher2026). Google Books record gives ISBN 9798985626636 and date 2026-02-17. Goodreads wrongly attaches this ISBN to the scripts book. The author's blog says it contains 6 scripts with time-sensitive versions and won a 2026 Living Now silver medal. Book page itself not opened here.
- **[@vedabharati2015]** Veda Bharati, Swami. *My Experiments With Yoga Nidra (excerpt titled 'My Experiments with Yoga Nidra: Inward Perceptions')*. Himalayan Yoga Publications Trust, Rishikesh. 2015. <https://www.amazon.in/dp/B00Y4X6XUU> (r3, confirmed, practitioner, cited ×1) — Kindle ASIN B00Y4X6XUU, 22 May 2015, 31 pp, 772 KB; Smashwords 21 May 2015, 6,400 words, $2.99; a hard-copy booklet was announced May 2015 from the HYPT office. No ISBN found. Published before the author's death (14 Jul 2015), so not posthumous. 2022 is only the date the excerpt was reposted on ahymsin.org; no 2022 print edition found. A short first-person story collection, anecdotal rather than how-to. Only a 358-word excerpt was read.
- **[@verma2021a]** Verma, T. *Yoga Nidra Scripts 2: More Meditations for Effortless Relaxation, Rejuvenation and Reconnection*. RTV Yoga Inc. 2021. <https://www.tamaraskyhawk.com/yns-2> (r2, confirmed, practitioner/popular) — Sequel to verma2021. ISBN 978-1-77748-882-6, 30 Apr 2021, 201 pp. (Open Library, Goodreads, AbeBooks); reissued under Tamara Skyhawk as '22 More Meditations...' ISBN 978-1-990622-11-3. 22 scripts, 15-45 min; themes include comfort, abundance, tension release, mornings, restful nights, confidence, creativity, Ayurvedic doshas; adds pranayama to the pre/post practices. Author page has an excerpt from 'Permission to Rest Yoga Nidra'. Goodreads 4.33/5 (24 ratings). https://www.goodreads.com/book/show/57927279-yoga-nidra-scripts-2
- **[@verma2021]** Verma, Tamara. *Yoga Nidra Scripts: 22 Meditations for Effortless Relaxation, Rejuvenation and Reconnection*. RTV Yoga Inc. (self-published). 2020. <https://www.goodreads.com/book/show/57212589> (r2, confirmed, practitioner/popular, cited ×3) — Vol. 1 first published December 2020 (AbeBooks ISBN 9781777488802: RTV Yoga Inc, 2020); vol. 2 (2021) and the vol. 1 hardcover are 2021. The id's 2021 is historical.
- **[@wildcroft2020]** Wildcroft, T. *Post-Lineage Yoga: From Guru to #MeToo*. Equinox. 2020. <https://theowildcroft.substack.com/p/read-how-to-write-a-book> (r4, read, scholarly/critical, cited ×1) — Wildcroft's PhD book on yoga after the guru model and abuse scandals; background to the book's anti-proprietary, safety-first stance. Seen only as the author's own reference; the publisher page returned 403.
- **[@wildcroft2026]** Wildcroft, T. & Nolan, B. *Leading Safe and Simple Yoga Nidras: A Yoga Teacher's Guide*. Singing Dragon (Jessica Kingsley Publishers / Hodder & Stoughton, Hachette UK). 2026. <https://uk.singingdragon.com/products/leading-safe-and-simple-yoga-nidras> (r4, confirmed, practitioner/scholarly, cited ×1) — Published 21 Aug 2026 (UK and US); 208 pp.; pb ISBN 9781399826280 (GBP 26.99 / USD 35), ebook ISBN 9781399826297. TOC seen on Google Books (id ftfzEQAAQBAJ, limited preview). QR-linked audio extracts + 3 full recorded practices with transcripts (incl. a two-voice nidra). Not yet in registry (selected.json registry_id null).
- **[@etevenon2006]** Étévenon, P. & Santerre, B. *États de conscience, sophrologie et yoga*. Tchou, Paris. 2006. <https://catalogue.bnf.fr/ark:/12148/cb409471402> (r3, confirmed, practitioner, fr) — 187 pp. The only book found that directly compares sophrology and yoga (Étévenon was an EEG researcher). Cited by Maruéjouls 2008 for EEG claims about the sophroliminal level. Existence confirmed in the BnF; not read. A lead for the comparison chapter.

## Book chapters

- **[@birch2018]** Birch, J. *The Proliferation of Asana-s in Late-Medieval Yoga Texts*. In: Yoga in Transformation (Vienna University Press / V&R unipress), pp. 101-180 (DOI 10.14220/9783737008624.101). 2018. <https://www.vr-elibrary.de/doi/pdf/10.14220/9783737008624.101> (r5, read, scholarly/historical) — Crossref metadata only; the PDF returned 403. It is the place to check how far later scholarship qualifies Yoga Body's claim that posture practice has little premodern precedent.
- **[@demichelis2020]** De Michelis, E. *The Modern Spirit of Yoga*. The Oxford History of Hinduism (ed. G. Flood), OUP, pp. 425-454. 2020. <https://doi.org/10.1093/oso/9780198733508.003.0017> (r5, confirmed, scholarly/historical) — The author's later overview, cited elsewhere for its discussion of 'healthism' in contemporary yoga. Not read; a possible update to the 2004 framework.
- **[@goldberg2013]** Goldberg, E. *Amrit Desai and the Kripalu Center for Yoga and Health, in Gleig, A. & Williamson, L. (eds.), Homegrown Gurus: From Hinduism in America to American Hinduism*. State University of New York Press. 2013. <https://openlibrary.org/works/OL22642815W> (r4, read, scholarly) — Seen only as a TOC entry on Open Library. The scholarly treatment of Amrit Desai, Kripalu and (presumably) the 1994 crisis; the Amrit Yoga Institute quotes this volume in its own promotion. Read it before writing about the Amrit lineage.
- **[@lusk2021h]** Julie Lusk. *Yoga Nidra Meditations: 24 Scripts for True Relaxation — publisher's 37-page excerpt (Contents, Acknowledgments, Introduction pp. 1-10, Ch. 1 pp. 13-19, back cover), page images*. Llewellyn Publications (Issuu account). 2021. <https://image.isu.pub/210618190054-113ed078a50e5739b39a494686b04b77/jpg/page_1.jpg> (r5, confirmed) — Pages 1-37 at .../jpg/page_N.jpg; front end https://issuu.com/llewellyn/docs/9780738764795. Shows printed Contents (12 Lusk + 12 contributed scripts), printed Acknowledgments without Miller, contraindications on Introduction p. 8.
- **[@sarkar2017]** Sarkar, B. *The Cult of Nidrā-Kālarātri, Goddess of Sleep, Swoon, Death and the Night (c. 3rd to 5th Century)*. In Heroic Shāktism: The Cult of Durgā in Ancient Indian Kingship, British Academy / Oxford UP. 2017. <https://doi.org/10.5871/bacad/9780197266106.003.0002> (r5, confirmed, scholarly/historical, cited ×3) — Indology: the sleep goddess cult behind the Devī Māhātmya's Yoganidrā; via compiler discover (crossref)
- **[@simmons2025]** Simmons, C. *Sleep Sweetly, Fierce Goddess*. In Sweetening and Intensification, pp. 191-214. 2025. <https://doi.org/10.2307/jj.33722020.12> (r4, confirmed, scholarly/cultural, cited ×1) — Goddess put to sleep in ritual; title only checked, author's first name not confirmed; via compiler discover (crossref)

## Papers

- **[@ahuja2024]** Ahuja, N.; Bhardwaj, P.; Pathania, M.; Sethi, D.; et al. *Yoga Nidra for hypertension: A systematic review and meta-analysis*. Journal of Ayurveda and Integrative Medicine 15(2):100882. 2024. <https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10950755/> (r4, confirmed, scholarly, cited ×1) — PMID 38484438. 5 RCTs + 3 non-RCTs, n=482. Pooled SBP -12.0 and DBP -6.3 mm Hg, but high risk of bias in RCTs and serious risk in 4 of 5 non-RCTs (incl. Devraj 2021), with I2 of 90-93%. Fills a gap in 06 sec. 6.0 (cardiovascular).
- **[@amita2009]** Amita, S. et al. *Effect of yoga-nidra on blood glucose level in diabetic patients*. Indian Journal of Physiology and Pharmacology 53(1):97-101. 2009. <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19810584/> (r2, read, scholarly, cited ×2)
- **[@nathoo2016a]** Ayesha Nathoo. *Hands of the Body? Relaxation therapy... (Nathoo 2016, Palgrave Communications, doi:10.1057/palcomms.2016.43)*. Palgrave Communications. 2016. <https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC4994869/fullTextXML> (r4, read, scholarly/historical) — Full title not checked; used for Call's background, Frances Archer's Nerve Training Colony and Jacobson's 'cult' criticism. Also cited in jacobson1929.md but not in the registry.
- **[@bali1979]** Bali, L. R. *Long-term effect of relaxation on blood pressure and anxiety levels of essential hypertensive males: a controlled study*. Psychosomatic Medicine 41(8):637-646. 1979. <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/397497/> (r3, confirmed, scholarly/critical) — Abstract read: generic 'relaxation', 18 hypertensive men, effects lasting 12 months; not described as yoga nidra. Handbook p. 118 cites it as 'Lekh Raj Bali (1997)' after 'yoga-nidra training'.
- **[@brownstein1989]** Brownstein, A. H.; Dembert, M. L. *Treatment of essential hypertension with yoga relaxation therapy in a USAF aviator: a case report*. Aviation, Space, and Environmental Medicine 60(7):684-687. 1989. <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/2669722/> (r3, confirmed, scholarly) — Single case of yoga relaxation training; the 'American pilot' anecdote in Kumar's Handbook p. 118 and ESR paper (where it is mis-cited as Bindu 1998).
- **[@brunner2008]** Brunner, J.; Schrempf, M.; Steger, F. *Johannes Heinrich Schultz and National Socialism*. Israel Journal of Psychiatry and Related Sciences 45(4):257-262. 2008. (r4, *unverified*, critical) — Cited via de.wikipedia; not opened. The scholarly source to use if the user's book mentions Schultz's Nazi-era record.
- **[@carrington1980]** Carrington, P.; Collings, G. H.; Benson, H. et al. *The use of meditation-relaxation techniques for the management of stress in a working population*. Journal of Occupational Medicine 22(4):221-231. 1980. <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/6988553/> (r4, confirmed, scholarly/critical) — Abstract read: CSM, respiratory one-method meditation and PMR vs waitlist in 154 New York Telephone employees; not yoga nidra. Handbook p. 118 presents it as a yoga nidra finding.
- **[@cooper1979]** Cooper, M. J.; Aygen, M. M. *A relaxation technique in the management of hypercholesterolemia*. Journal of Human Stress 5(4):24-27. 1979. <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/392003/> (r3, confirmed, scholarly/critical) — Abstract read: tested Transcendental Meditation (12 vs 11 hypercholesterolaemic subjects), not yoga nidra and not cardiac patients. Repeatedly cited in yoga nidra literature (Kumar 2008, 2012, Handbook p. 57) as a yoga nidra cholesterol study - useful for a 'recycled citations' warning.
- **[@cramer2018]** Cramer, H., Anheyer, D., Saha, F.J., Dobos, G. *Yoga for posttraumatic stress disorder - a systematic review and meta-analysis*. BMC Psychiatry. 2018. <https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5863799/> (r4, confirmed, scholarly) — 7 RCTs, none of them iRest or yoga nidra. Low-quality evidence vs. no treatment, very low vs. attention control; 'only a weak recommendation for yoga as an adjunctive intervention for PTSD'. Useful to check the 'proven-effective' claim.
- **[@datta2022]** Datta, K. et al. *Electrophysiological evidence of local sleep during yoga nidra practice*. Frontiers in Neurology 13:910794. 2022. <https://doi.org/10.3389/fneur.2022.910794> (r3, read, scholarly, cited ×1)
- **[@datta2021]** Datta, K. et al. *Yoga nidra practice shows improvement in sleep in patients with chronic insomnia: a randomized controlled trial*. National Medical Journal of India 34(3):143-150. 2021. <https://doi.org/10.25259/NMJI_63_19> (r3, read, scholarly, cited ×2) — RCT n=41: yoga nidra (21) vs CBT-I (20); both improved
- **[@demichelis2007]** De Michelis, E. *A Preliminary Survey of Modern Yoga Studies*. Asian Medicine 3(1):1-19 (Brill). 2007. <https://doi.org/10.1163/157342107X207182> (r5, confirmed, scholarly/historical) — The author's own restatement and revision of her 2004 typology. It gives p. 188 of the book for the typology (n. 35), revises it to five types and names Sivananda's pupils, the Himalayan Institute and Kripalu as psychosomatic yoga (n. 25). It gives a post-1950 chronology (popularisation, consolidation, acculturation; ch. 6) and credits Singleton 2005 with the history of relaxation. Pp. 1-12 read via a PDF linked from Wikipedia; the host is probably the Modern Yoga Research site but unconfirmed, so cite by DOI.
- **[@devraj2021]** Devraj, J. P.; Santosh Kumar, B.; Raja Sriswan, M.; et al. *Effect of Yoganidra on Blood Pressure, Hs-CRP, and Lipid Profile of Hypertensive Subjects: A Pilot Study*. Evidence-Based Complementary and Alternative Medicine 2021:2858235 (ICMR-National Institute of Nutrition, Hyderabad). 2021. <https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8739171/> (r3, confirmed, scholarly) — PMID 35003295, doi 10.1155/2021/2858235, CC BY. Non-randomised (self-selected yoga nidra group n=31 vs no-intervention controls n=43), unblinded, 12 weeks, 35-min recorded protocol, Satyananda-format stages. SBP 142.9 to 118.7 vs 134.3 to 130.7; hs-CRP down. Baseline imbalance; participants halved antihypertensives in weeks 4-5. Funded ('logistics and financial support') by Rishi Chaitanya Ashram (Gurumaa), yet declares no conflicts. Basis of the book's ch. 7 and appendix. Rated serious risk of bias by Ahuja 2024.
- **[@eastmanmueller2013]** Eastman-Mueller, H., Wilson, T., Jung, A.K., Kimura, A. & Tarrant, J. *iRest yoga-nidra on the college campus: changes in stress, depression, worry, and mindfulness*. International Journal of Yoga Therapy 23(2):15-24. 2013. <https://doi.org/10.17761/ijyt.23.2.r8735770101m8277> (r3, read, scholarly, cited ×2) — Higher-education setting; via compiler discover [read: abstract only]
- **[@ferreiravorkapic2018]** Ferreira-Vorkapic, C. et al. *The Impact of Yoga Nidra and Seated Meditation on the Mental Health of College Professors*. International Journal of Yoga 11(3):215-223. 2018. <https://doi.org/10.4103/ijoy.IJOY_57_17> (r3, read, scholarly, cited ×1)
- **[@fialoke2024]** Fialoke, S., Tripathi, V., Thakral, S., Dhawan, A. et al. *Functional connectivity changes in meditators and novices during yoga nidra practice*. Scientific Reports 14. 2024. <https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-024-63765-7> (r4, read, scholarly, cited ×5) — fMRI; Huberman publicised it on X as showing NSDR 'is not just a light nap'; via web search [read: abstract only]
- **[@gibbs2025]** Gibbs, V., Hanney, W.J. & Anderson, A.W. *Comparing the Effects of Yoga Nidra and Body Scan Meditation on Pain Outcomes*. International Journal of Yoga 18(3):342-347. 2025. <https://doi.org/10.4103/ijoy.ijoy_2_25> (r3, read, scholarly, cited ×1) — Head-to-head with a secular body scan; via compiler discover [read: abstract only]
- **[@gomathy2026]** Gomathy, S.B., Sharma, S., Sharma, G. et al. *Efficacy of adjunctive Yoga Nidra in patients with functional dissociative seizures receiving structured psychoeducation (YOGA-FDS): a pilot randomised controlled trial*. Epilepsy & Behavior 183:111180. 2026. <https://doi.org/10.1016/j.yebeh.2026.111180> (r3, read, scholarly, cited ×2) — AIIMS New Delhi; relevant to the 'dissociation' caution in ch. 07; via compiler discover [read: abstract only]
- **[@grossman2023]** Grossman, P. *Fundamental challenges and likely refutations of the five basic premises of the polyvagal theory*. Biological Psychology 180:108589. 2023. <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37230290/> (r4, confirmed, scholarly/critical) — Argues each premise of Porges' polyvagal theory is untenable or highly implausible. Relevant whenever yoga nidra books use polyvagal language for the nervous system. Abstract only.
- **[@grossman2026]** Grossman, P. *Why The Polyvagal Theory Is Untenable: An international expert evaluation of the polyvagal theory and commentary upon Porges (2025)*. Clinical Neuropsychiatry 23(1):100-112. 2026. <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41768017/> (r4, confirmed, scholarly/critical) — 39-author expert evaluation (Grossman, Ackland, Berntson, Gourine, Joyner, Khalsa, Paton and others) rejecting polyvagal theory. Pairs with Grossman 2023. Not yet covered in the compendium, which has no polyvagal section. Abstract and author list only.
- **[@gunnarsdottir2022]** Gunnarsdottir, T.J., Sigurðardottir, S. & Jonsdottir, H. *For the First Time I Felt Great Hope: Experience of Yoga and Yoga Nidra as Therapy for Depression, Anxiety and Stress*. Journal of Yoga and Physiotherapy 9(3). 2022. <https://doi.org/10.19080/jyp.2022.09.555763> (r2, confirmed, scholarly/cultural, cited ×2) — Icelandic nursing researchers; qualitative, patients' own words; low-impact venue; via compiler discover (crossref)
- **[@hoye2016]** Hoye, S. & Reddy, S. *Yoga-nidra and hypnosis*. International Journal of Health Promotion and Education 54(3):117-125. 2016. <https://doi.org/10.1080/14635240.2016.1142061> (r3, confirmed, scholarly, cited ×3) — via web search; publisher page paywalled
- **[@husmann2017]** Husmann, B. *Die Entwicklung des Autogenen Trainings im Rahmen von Zeitgeschichte und beruflicher Karriere von Prof. Dr. Johannes Heinrich Schultz 1902-32*. Entspannungsverfahren 34, 24-118 (DG-E / Pabst). 2017. <https://www.bjoern-husmann.de/download/file/41-husmann-b-2017-die-entwicklung-des-autogenen-trainings-im-rahmen-von-zeitgeschichte-und-beruflicher-karriere-von-prof-dr-johannes-heinrich-schultz-1902-32> (r4, read, historical, de) — Abstract only. It covers the development of AT up to the 1932 monograph, Nazi-era influences in later editions, and Schultz's contact with yoga before 1932. The full PDF is free from the author's site after a personal-use licence click-through, which I did not accept on the user's behalf. Husmann's other articles (2015, 2016, 2018) are listed on de.wikipedia; the 2018 English PDF link on dg-e.de returned 404.
- **[@andrick2012]** John M. Andrick. *Delsartean hypnosis for girls' bodies and minds: Annie Payson Call and the Lasell Seminary nerve training controversy*. History of Psychology 15(2):124-144, doi:10.1037/a0023682. 2012. <https://doi.org/10.1037/a0023682> (r4, read, scholarly/historical, cited ×2) — Abstract only (PMID 22849003): the 1890 Lasell hypnosis controversy; Call's method as 'Delsartean hypnosis' and a model for later relaxation training.
- **[@kjaer2002]** Kjaer, T.W. et al. *Increased dopamine tone during meditation-induced change of consciousness*. Cognitive Brain Research 13(2):255-259. 2002. <https://doi.org/10.1016/s0926-6410(01)00106-9> (r4, read, scholarly, cited ×7)
- **[@kumar2008]** Kumar, K. *A study on the impact on stress and anxiety through Yoga nidra*. Indian Journal of Traditional Knowledge 7(3):401-404 (NISCAIR). 2008. <https://nopr.niscpr.res.in/handle/123456789/1700> (r2, confirmed, scholarly/data) — Free full text on NOPR. 80 DSVV PG yoga students (yoga + 30 min YN daily, 6 months) vs 30 yoga-only controls, quota allocation; Eight State Questionnaire; post-test means only; t=2.5 (anxiety), 2.48 (stress) 'at 0.01'. Attributes Cooper & Aygen (TM) cholesterol result to yoga nidra; cites Satyananda as designer of the modern form.
- **[@kumar2009]** Kumar, K.; Joshi, B. *Study on the effect of Pranakarshan pranayama and Yoga nidra on alpha EEG & GSR*. Indian Journal of Traditional Knowledge 8(3):453-454 (NISCAIR). 2009. <https://nopr.niscpr.res.in/handle/123456789/5093> (r1, confirmed, scholarly/data) — Free full text on NOPR. 40 college students, yoga nidra (30 min) + Pranakarshan pranayama (15 min) for 40 days, pre-post, no control; alpha EEG and GSR changes; concludes improved 'immunity' (non sequitur). Example of weak early Indian YN literature.
- **[@kumar2012]** Kumar, K.; Pandya, P. *A study on the impact on ESR level through Yogic Relaxation Technique Yoga nidra*. Indian Journal of Traditional Knowledge 11(2):358-361 (NISCAIR). 2012. <https://nopr.niscpr.res.in/handle/123456789/13871> (r2, confirmed, scholarly/data) — Free full text on NOPR. Same DSVV sample (80 vs 30). Post ESR values compared; female result (10.40+-11.10 vs 12.15+-13.95) reported as t=2.82 at 0.01, which the printed figures do not support. Interprets lower ESR in healthy students as better immunity via iron absorption. Basis of book ch. 12.
- **[@kumari2025]** Kumari, R., Saxena, V., Rao, R. et al. *Efficacy of yoga nidra compared with sleep education on sleep quality among medical students at a tertiary healthcare centre in Rishikesh, Uttarakhand, India: protocol for a randomised controlled trial*. BMJ Open 15(10):e103725. 2025. <https://doi.org/10.1136/bmjopen-2025-103725> (r3, read, scholarly/data, cited ×1) — Protocol; registered CTRI/2022/07/044426 (AIIMS Rishikesh) [read: abstract only]
- **[@lehrer2025]** Lehrer, P. *The Importance of Including Psychophysiological Methods in Psychotherapy*. Applied Psychophysiology and Biofeedback 50(2):169-188. 2025. <https://doi.org/10.1007/s10484-024-09667-w> (r4, confirmed, scholarly) — Open access (PMC12081504, CC BY). Author trained under Jacobson. Describes the original method from the 1938 edition: tension used only as a teaching device, 'method of diminishing tensions' (pp. 185-186), 'go negative', no suggestion, eye and speech muscles and mental activity (ch. 10), differential relaxation (ch. 6). Argues that the later tense-hard-release version (Bernstein & Borkovec) is less sound. Best free description of Jacobson's actual method.
- **[@lehrer1982]** Lehrer, P. M. *How to relax and how not to relax: a re-evaluation of the work of Edmund Jacobson - I*. Behaviour Research and Therapy 20(5):417-428. 1982. <https://doi.org/10.1016/0005-7967(82)90063-8> (r4, read, critical) — Classic critique comparing Jacobson's original method with abbreviated PMR. PubMed record only (no abstract); not read.
- **[@lindahl2017]** Lindahl, J.R., Fisher, N.E., Cooper, D.J., Rosen, R.K. & Britton, W.B. *The varieties of contemplative experience: A mixed-methods study of meditation-related challenges in Western Buddhists*. PLoS ONE 12(5):e0176239. 2017. <https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0176239> (r5, read, scholarly/critical, CC BY 4.0, cited ×1) — Religious studies + psychiatry; catalogue of challenging meditation experiences [read: abstract only]
- **[@livingston2018]** Livingston, E. & Collette-Merrill, K. *Effectiveness of Integrative Restoration (iRest) Yoga Nidra on Mindfulness, Sleep, and Pain in Health Care Workers*. Holistic Nursing Practice 32(3):160-166. 2018. <https://doi.org/10.1097/hnp.0000000000000266> (r3, read, scholarly, cited ×2) — Nursing journal; via compiler discover (Europe PMC) [read: abstract only]
- **[@loftus2026]** Loftus, T., Yau, S.H., Soares, S., Byrne, J. & Mathersul, D.C. *Co-designed yoga nidra targeting anxiety in autistic children: A mixed methods feasibility study*. Research in Developmental Disabilities 176:105367. 2026. <https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ridd.2026.105367> (r3, read, scholarly/community, cited ×2) — Co-designed with autistic children and families (community input); via compiler discover [read: abstract only]
- **[@lou1999]** Lou, H.C. et al. *A 15O-H2O PET study of meditation and the resting state of normal consciousness*. Human Brain Mapping 7(2):98-105. 1999. <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/9950067/> (r4, read, scholarly, cited ×7)
- **[@luu2024]** Luu, K. *Key Components of Trauma-Informed Yoga Nidra*. International Journal of Yoga Therapy 34. 2024. <https://doi.org/10.17761/2024-d-24-00021> (r3, read, scholarly, cited ×5) — via compiler discover (crossref) [read: abstract only]
- **[@moszeik2026]** Moszeik, E. N.; El-Mahdy, C. *Unpacking the Mechanisms: How Mindfulness Facets Shape Subjective and Biological Responses to Yoga Nidra Meditation*. Mindfulness 17(3):893-904 (Springer), CC BY 4.0. 2026. <https://doi.org/10.1007/s12671-026-02773-2> (r4, read, scholarly) — Secondary analysis of the same trial (N = 255, the three intervention groups only). Trait mindfulness facets (Describing, Nonjudging) moderate the yoga nidra effects. Not preregistered. Only the abstract was read, via Crossref.
- **[@moszeik2025a]** Moszeik, E. N.; Renner, K.-H. *The conscientious meditator - Practice, practice, all is coming? The moderating role of personality in an experimental intervention study with yoga nidra meditation*. Psychology of Consciousness: Theory, Research, and Practice (APA), CC BY-NC-ND 4.0. 2025. <https://doi.org/10.1037/cns0000444> (r4, read, scholarly) — Published version of the dissertation's personality-moderator analyses; conscientiousness is the main amplifier. Only the Crossref record was seen; PsycNet did not load.
- **[@moszeik2025]** Moszeik, E.N., Rohleder, N. & Renner, K.H. *The Effects of an Online Yoga Nidra Meditation on Subjective Well-Being and Diurnal Salivary Cortisol: A Randomised Controlled Trial*. Stress and Health 41(3):e70049. 2025. <https://doi.org/10.1002/smi.70049> (r4, read, scholarly, cited ×9) — German research group (Universität der Bundeswehr München / Erlangen); via compiler discover [read: abstract only]
- **[@moszeik2020]** Moszeik, E.N., von Oertzen, T. & Renner, K.H. *Effectiveness of a short Yoga Nidra meditation on stress, sleep, and well-being in a large and diverse sample*. Current Psychology 41(8):5272-5286 (online 2020, print 2022). 2020. <https://doi.org/10.1007/s12144-020-01042-2> (r4, read, scholarly, cited ×1) — 11-min online practice; N=341 vs waitlist N=430; very small to small effects
- **[@myga2022]** Myga, K. A.; Kuehn, E.; Azañón, E. *Autosuggestion: a cognitive process that empowers your brain?* Experimental Brain Research 240: 381-394; doi:10.1007/s00221-021-06265-8. 2022. <https://europepmc.org/article/PMC/PMC8858297> (r4, confirmed, scholarly) — Open-access review; full text read. Concludes there is little empirical evidence on autosuggestion. Proposes that experimental autosuggestions refer to 'I', state the outcome in the present tense, are short, and avoid negations. Useful modern check for sankalpa-wording rules and for the hypnosis/autogenic comparison. Volume/page numbers not checked.
- **[@nassif2015]** Nassif, T.H. et al. *Mindfulness meditation and chronic pain management in Iraq and Afghanistan veterans with traumatic brain injury: A pilot study*. Military Behavioral Health 4(1):82-89. 2015. <https://doi.org/10.1080/21635781.2015.1119772> (r2, read, scholarly, cited ×2) — Tiny pilot: iRest n=4 vs TAU n=5, 8 weeks
- **[@nathoo2016]** Nathoo, A. *Initiating therapeutic relaxation in Britain: a twentieth-century strategy for health and wellbeing*. Palgrave Communications 2:16043. 2016. <https://doi.org/10.1057/palcomms.2016.43> (r4, confirmed, historical) — Open access (PMC4994869). History of relaxation therapy from Jacobson and Annie Payson Call to the British 'Relaxation for Living' charity (1972). Quotes 'You Must Relax' (1934) pp. 8, 43, 44, 126; says Jacobson distanced his method from hypnosis, mysticism and Schultz; summarises Singleton 2005 (modern yoga's relaxationism owes more to Call and Jacobson than to ancient tradition). Covers antenatal relaxation and yoga classes in Britain.
- **[@parker2019]** Parker, S. *Training attention for conscious non-REM sleep: The yogic practice of yoga-nidrā and its implications for neuroscience research*. Progress in Brain Research 244:255-272. 2019. <https://doi.org/10.1016/bs.pbr.2018.10.016> (r4, read, scholarly/practitioner, cited ×4) — Himalayan-tradition teacher and psychologist; via web search [read: abstract only]
- **[@parker2013]** Parker, S., Bharati, S.V. & Fernandez, M. *Defining Yoga-Nidra: Traditional Accounts, Physiological Research, and Future Directions*. International Journal of Yoga Therapy 23(1):11-16. 2013. <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24016819/> (r4, read, scholarly/practitioner, cited ×8)
- **[@pence2014]** Pence, P.G. et al. *Delivering Integrative Restoration-Yoga Nidra Meditation (iRest) to women with sexual trauma at a veteran's medical center: a pilot study*. International Journal of Yoga Therapy 24:53-62. 2014. <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25858651/> (r3, read, scholarly, cited ×3)
- **[@rani2011]** Rani, K. et al. *Impact of yoga nidra on psychological general wellbeing in patients with menstrual irregularities: a randomized controlled trial*. International Journal of Yoga 4(1):20-25. 2011. <https://doi.org/10.4103/0973-6131.78176> (r3, read, scholarly, cited ×1)
- **[@rani2013]** Rani, M. *Impact of Yoga Nidra on menstrual abnormalities in females of reproductive age*. Journal of Alternative and Complementary Medicine 19(12):925-929. 2013. <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23647406/> (r3, confirmed, scholarly) — RCT, Lucknow; 150 randomised, 126 completed; 35-40 min/day, 5x/week, 6 months vs no-intervention control; TSH, FSH, LH and prolactin decreased. Cited in Göttinger 2025 as 'confirming' hormonal benefit. Distinct from rani2011 (psychological wellbeing, Int J Yoga). Abstract only.
- **[@roenneberg2007]** Roenneberg, T. et al. *Epidemiology of the human circadian clock*. Sleep Medicine Reviews 11: 429-438. 2007. (r4, *unverified*, scholarly) — Cited by Wildcroft as a source for the book's sleep chapters (chronotypes). Not opened.
- **[@sarang2006]** Sarang, P. S. & Telles, S. *Oxygen consumption and respiration during and after two yoga relaxation techniques*. Applied Psychophysiology and Biofeedback 31(2):143-53. 2006. <https://doi.org/10.1007/s10484-006-9012-8> (r3, read) — Abstract only (PMID 16838123). O2 -19.3% after CM vs -4.8% after supine rest, n=50 men.
- **[@singleton2007]** Singleton, M. *Suggestive Therapeutics: New Thought's Relationship to Modern Yoga*. Asian Medicine 3(1): 64-84 (DOI 10.1163/157342107x207218). 2007. <https://brill.com/view/journals/asme/3/1/article-p64_6.xml> (r4, confirmed, scholarly/historical) — Abstract read via Crossref; the Brill page returned 403. New Thought (positive thinking, personal power, healing) shaped early-20th-century transnational yoga. Context for sankalpa as autosuggestion and for debate 10 (is it hypnosis?).
- **[@singleton2005]** Singleton, Mark. *Salvation through Relaxation: Proprioceptive Therapy and its Relationship to Yoga*. Journal of Contemporary Religion 20(3): 289-304. 2005. <https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/abs/10.1080/13537900500249780> (r4, confirmed, scholarly/historical/critical, cited ×17) — Shows modern yogic relaxation (incl. yoga nidra-type practice) draws on 19th-20th c. Western relaxationism
- **[@stankovic2011]** Stankovic, L. *Transforming trauma: a qualitative feasibility study of integrative restoration (iRest) yoga Nidra on combat-related post-traumatic stress disorder*. International Journal of Yoga Therapy (21):23-37. 2011. <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22398342/> (r3, read, scholarly, cited ×3)
- **[@stetter2002]** Stetter, F.; Kupper, S. *Autogenic training: a meta-analysis of clinical outcome studies*. Applied Psychophysiology and Biofeedback 27(1):45-98. 2002. <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12001885/> (r4, confirmed, scholarly) — Abstract read: 60 controlled studies (35 RCTs). Medium effects vs control conditions; mostly no effect or small negative effects vs other psychological treatments. This matches the yoga nidra pattern in 06 §6.0.
- **[@telles2000]** Telles, S., Reddy, S. K. & Nagendra, H. R. *Oxygen consumption and respiration following two yoga relaxation techniques*. Applied Psychophysiology and Biofeedback 25(4):221-7. 2000. <https://doi.org/10.1023/a:1026454804927> (r3, read) — Abstract only (PMID 11218923). CM vs Savasana: O2 -32.1% vs -10.1%, n=40 men; SVYASA in-house study.
- **[@tyagi2025]** Tyagi, P.; Jain, K. *Effects of yogic practices on physiological and biochemical parameters of hypertensive patients: A systematic review of clinical trials*. Journal of Ayurveda and Integrative Medicine. 2025. <https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12145764/> (r3, read, scholarly) — PMID 40378653. Only the reference list was checked (it includes Devraj 2021 and Anjana 2022); findings not read.
- **[@vandam2018]** Van Dam, N.T., van Vugt, M.K., Vago, D.R., Schmalzl, L. et al. *Mind the Hype: A Critical Evaluation and Prescriptive Agenda for Research on Mindfulness and Meditation*. Perspectives on Psychological Science 13(1):36-61. 2018. <https://doi.org/10.1177/1745691617709589> (r5, read, scholarly/critical, cited ×1) — Critique of the meditation research field; applies directly to yoga nidra claims [read: abstract only]
- **[@witkowski2010]** Witkowski, T. *Thirty-Five Years of Research on Neuro-Linguistic Programming. NLP Research Data Base. State of the Art or Pseudoscientific Decoration?* Polish Psychological Bulletin 41(2). 2010. <https://doi.org/10.2478/v10059-010-0008-0> (r4, confirmed, scholarly/critical) — Abstract read via OpenAlex. Of 33 analysed studies, 18.2% support NLP tenets, 54.5% do not and 27.3% are uncertain; concludes that the results contradict NLP's claimed empirical basis. Use it to check NLP-based yoga nidra claims (Sistema Darshan). OA PDF: http://journals.pan.pl/Content/99644/PDF/03.pdf (not opened).
- **[@wood2009]** Wood, J. V.; Perunovic, W. Q. E.; Lee, J. W. *Positive self-statements: power for some, peril for others*. Psychological Science 20(7): 860-866; doi:10.1111/j.1467-9280.2009.02370.x. 2009. <https://europepmc.org/article/MED/19493324> (r4, read, scholarly) — Abstract only. People with low self-esteem who repeated 'I'm a lovable person' felt worse; those with high self-esteem felt slightly better. Key caution for affirmation-style sankalpas; supports progressive or modest wording. Volume/pages from memory; verify.
- **[@yeates2016]** Yeates, L. B. *Émile Coué and his Method (III): Every Day in Every Way*. Australian Journal of Clinical Hypnotherapy & Hypnosis 38(1): 55-79; open access on UNSWorks (CC BY-NC-ND). 2016. <https://unsworks.unsw.edu.au/handle/1959.4/unsworks_47938> (r4, confirmed, scholarly) — Pp. 1-16 read. Argues Coué used hypnotism all his life. His 1912 lecture already asked patients to fall asleep thinking the words. The English formula reached its final form ('Every day, in every way...') only in 1923. Links the 20-knot string and ritual to the Orthodox prayer rope / Jesus Prayer (the author's inference). Says Coué dropped 'think of the operator' by January 1923. Parts I and II (2016a, 2016b) are on UNSWorks too.
- **[@zaccaro2021]** Zaccaro, A., Riehl, A., Piarulli, A., Alfì, G. et al. *The Consciousness State of Traditional Nidrâ Yoga/Modern Yoga Nidra: Phenomenological Characterization and Preliminary Insights from an EEG Study*. International Journal of Yoga Therapy 31(1). 2021. <https://doi.org/10.17761/2021-d-20-00014> (r4, read, scholarly/cultural, cited ×12) — Italian (Pisa) group; distinguishes a 'traditional Nidrâ Yoga' from modern Yoga Nidra; via compiler discover (crossref) [read: abstract only]

## Reviews

- **[@customers2021]** Agapea customers. *Yoga-nidra (Agapea reader opinions)*. Agapea. 2021. <https://www.agapea.com/Vicente-Merlo-Lillo/Yoga-nidra-9788499889108-i.htm> (r1, read, community, es) — Four enthusiastic short reviews, Oct-Nov 2021, probably from readers close to the author ('the reward for 6 years of work'). Lists Vicente Merlo Lillo as co-contributor (prologue). Bookseller data: 354 pp., paperback with flaps, 29/09/2021.
- **[@albahari2015]** Albahari, M. *Review of Waking, Dreaming, Being (Notre Dame Philosophical Reviews 2015.07.15)*. Notre Dame Philosophical Reviews. 2015. <https://ndpr.nd.edu/reviews/waking-dreaming-being-self-and-consciousness-in-neuroscience-meditation-and-philosophy/> (r4, confirmed, scholarly/critical) — Read in full. Calls ch. 8 'highly innovative' but objects that the meta-awareness of trained sleep yogis may create a mode of awareness not normally present, not reveal that ordinary deep sleep is conscious. Also critiques the no-self chapter.
- **[@altglas2005]** Altglas, V. *Elisabeth De Michelis, A History of Modern Yoga. Patanjali and Western Esotericism (compte rendu)*. Archives de sciences sociales des religions 131-132 (OpenEdition). 2005. <https://journals.openedition.org/assr/3121> (r4, read, scholarly, fr) — Open-access French review, seen only through a fetch-tool summary because the site's bot filter blocked direct reading. It calls the book an essential contribution; modern yoga as tied to health, well-being and individual self-improvement. No criticisms reported.
- **[@stoma1082015]** Amazon customers (incl. 'Stoma108'). *Amazon customer reviews of My Experiments With Yoga Nidra (Kindle)*. Amazon. 2015-2020. <https://www.amazon.co.uk/dp/B00Y4X6XUU> (r2, confirmed, community) — 3.5/5 from 100 ratings (17% one-star). The 'Stoma108' review (2016), presumably Stephen Parker, frames the book as a phenomenological account of the delta state and claims lab measurements showed Swami Veda 'perpetually' in that state. One reviewer says it was required reading for Indu Arora's yoga nidra training.
- **[@customers2012]** Amazon.de customers. *Amazon.de customer reviews of Skuban, Yoga Nidra (2012 and 2023 editions)*. Amazon.de. 2012-2026. <https://www.amazon.de/dp/3442341140> (r2, read, community, de) — 2012: 4.3/5 from 45 ratings; 2023 (https://www.amazon.de/dp/3894279222): 4.6/5 from 20. Readers confirm no sankalpa, almost no visualisation, reorderable CD sections, two practices; criticisms: speaker's mouth noises (2012), no printed script and download problems (2023).
- **[@customers2018]** Amazon.es customers. *Customer reviews of 'Yoga Nidra + Mindfulness' (Amazon.es), with Goodreads rating*. Amazon.es; Goodreads. 2018-2024. <https://www.amazon.es/dp/8491112650> (r1, read, community, es) — Amazon.es: 20 ratings, average 4.0 (64% five stars, 15% four, 21% one). The verified one-star reviews call it a promotional 'leaflet' for the Darshan system and a 'plagiarism' of ordinary yoga nidra, and say the CD does not relax. Goodreads (https://www.goodreads.com/book/show/38084126-yoga-nidra-mindfulness): one 2-star rating, no text reviews.
- **[@anon1987]** Anon. *Review of Hypnagogia, by Andreas Mavromatis (Routledge & Kegan Paul 1987)*. Psychological Medicine 17(4):1029. 1987. <https://doi.org/10.1017/s0033291700001318> (r3, confirmed, critical) — Unsigned on the visible page. Brief: wide literature in several languages; many observations, much speculation, few conclusions.
- **[@anon1991]** Anon. *Review of Hypnagogia, by A. Mavromatis (Routledge 1991 paperback)*. Psychological Medicine 21(3):804. 1991. <https://doi.org/10.1017/s0033291700022777> (r3, confirmed, critical) — Unsigned on the visible page. Calls it a long, rambling monograph adapted from a 1983 doctoral thesis; many unusual facts and suggestive speculations, only tentative conclusions.
- **[@sridharan2023]** Anuradha Sridharan. *Book Review: Yoga for Women (Nawa Yogini Tantra)*. 2023. <https://www.anuradhasridharan.com/2023/01/book-review-yoga-for-women-nawa-yogini-tantra.html> (r2, read, popular)
- **[@arewa2025]** Arewa, C. S. *Nidra Shakti: The Power of Rest – A Groundbreaking Mother-Piece*. Yoga Magazine (UK), January 2025 issue. 2025. <https://www.yoganidranetwork.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/02/Yoga-Magazine-Jan-25-NS.pdf> (r2, confirmed, popular) — The only substantive published review found. It is enthusiastic and not independent: the reviewer says she was interviewed for the book. Gives the release as November 2024, 'over 700 pages'. Mentions colour plates (Vishnu in yoga nidra), the A-Z, 108 transcripts, an ethics section, a research review covering about 50 years, and Xhosa, Aboriginal Dreamtime and Celtic dream practices. Two-page scanned PDF hosted by the publisher.
- **[@arya2025]** Arya, R.G., Srivastava, D., Divya, B.R., Madhu & Bhargav, H. *A Systematic Review of Yoga Interventions on the Mental Health of Nursing Professionals and Students*. International Journal of Yoga. 2025. <https://doi.org/10.4103/ijoy.ijoy_195_24> (r3, confirmed, scholarly, cited ×1) — Covers yoga broadly, incl. yoga nidra; via compiler discover (Europe PMC)
- **[@barnes2023]** Barnes, J. *The Gospel of the Nap Bishop*. South Side Weekly. 2023. <https://southsideweekly.com/the-gospel-of-the-nap-bishop/> (r3, confirmed, cultural) — 30 July 2023 review-profile. Reports that the four parts (REST!, DREAM!, RESIST!, IMAGINE!) each centre on one of the four tenets, and that the book has journaling questions and meditations. Gives Chicago background.
- **[@bond1987]** Bond, A. *Review of Hypnogogia [sic]: The Unique State of Consciousness Between Wakefulness and Sleep, by Andreas Mavromatis*. British Journal of Psychiatry 151(6):877. 1987. <https://doi.org/10.1192/s0007125000137456> (r3, confirmed, scholarly) — Short, favourable review (read on Cambridge first-page preview): timely, fascinating for lay and specialist readers; summarises three-part structure and author's view that the state can be entered deliberately, is usually pleasant and can be therapeutic.
- **[@mascarello2016]** Chiara Mascarello. *Review of Evan Thompson, Waking, Dreaming, Being*. Universa. Recensioni di filosofia 5:2, pp. 160-165. 2016. <https://universa.padovauniversitypress.it/system/files/papers/05-2-31.pdf> (r4, confirmed) — Open-access review summarising chapters 1-10, with page-cited quotes from ch. 4 (p. 122) and ch. 8 (pp. 248, 260, 268).
- **[@cobb2019]** Cobb, D. J. *Daring to Rest, by Karen Brody (review of the audio programme)*. Spiral Nature Magazine. 2019. <https://www.spiralnature.com/reviews/daring-to-rest-karen-brody/> (r2, confirmed, community) — First-person review by a reviewer with complex PTSD. It reviews the 2018 AUDIO programme (ISBN 9781683640691), not the book. Reports 8 meditations x 5 days, sankalpa repeated at beginning/middle/end, soul whispers, free writing, a touchstone held in the left hand. Lasting benefit reported; little felt at wisdom/bliss levels.
- **[@coles2018]** Coles, D. *What is mindfulness? Five book reviews (includes Yoga Nidra Meditation)*. Spiral Nature Magazine. 2018. <https://www.spiralnature.com/reviews/what-is-mindfulness-five-book-reviews/> (r2, confirmed, community) — 12 Feb 2018. Short review. Many pages on Hindu belief; the practice has 'many, many steps' and is perhaps best guided by someone else; an advanced practice.
- **[@coward2005]** Coward, H. *Book Review: "A History of Modern Yoga: Patanjali and Western Esotericism"*. Journal of Hindu-Christian Studies 18(1). 2005. <https://doi.org/10.7825/2164-6279.1348> (r4, confirmed, scholarly) — Open-access review. Only the landing page was opened; the PDF returned 403 in this session. Per Wikipedia it stresses the gap between Patanjali's Yoga Sutras and Vivekananda's reinterpretation.
- **[@coward2010]** Coward, H. *Book Review: Yoga Body: The Origins of Modern Posture Practice*. - **[@users2020]** Goodreads users. *Goodreads: Yoga-Nidra – 108 pratiques à conjuguer pour s'éveiller à l'infini*. - **[@users2026b]** Goodreads users. *Goodreads: The Book of Yoga Nidra Meditation Scripts (ratings and community reviews)*. Goodreads. 2026. <https://www.goodreads.com/book/show/200733022-the-book-of-yoga-nidra-meditation-scripts> (r2, confirmed, community) — 4.63 from 73 ratings, 32 text reviews (2 Oct 2026). All the text reviews are enthusiastic. 8 of them were posted in launch week, and one ('Linda') is word for word the endorsement by Linda Snay. Useful content details: scripts 25-45 min; every script has a body scan; per-script theme, timing, complementary inspiration and preparations.

Goodreads. 2021-2025. <https://www.goodreads.com/book/show/55410533-yoga-nidra-meditations> (r2, confirmed, community) — 4.37 from 41 ratings and 6 reviews (Oct 2026). Dawn Thomas gives the Part and chapter titles; Jamie Bee mentions four appendices (brain waves, chakras, leading groups); Lady Katie criticises the 'more and more' refrain and the inclusion of 'gurus' with abuse allegations, and notes contraindications.

Describes the three parts and four appendices; mentions colour brain-scan plates; recommends it as a teacher's reference; finds it repetitive and dry in places. Misnames the author (a Goodreads metadata error).

Confirms the four elements (quiet environment, repeated word, passive attitude, comfortable position) and 10-20 min twice daily; says the steps owe much to TM and religious practice; calls Benson careful in his claims.
- **[@reviews1977]** Kirkus Reviews. *Beyond Biofeedback (Kirkus review)*. Kirkus Reviews, 1 July 1977 issue. 1977. <https://www.kirkusreviews.com/book-reviews/elmer-alyce-green/beyond-biofeedback/> (r3, confirmed, popular/critical) — Contemporary trade review: values the observations and the India chapter; readers need not follow the syncretic beliefs, ESP and mysticism; the book does not explain how or why the methods work.
- **[@konecki2011]** Konecki, K. T. *Book Review: Yoga Body. The Origins of Modern Posture Practice by Marc Singleton*. Qualitative Sociology Review 7(3): 97-101 (DOI 10.18778/1733-8077.7.3.05). 2011. <https://czasopisma.uni.lodz.pl/qualit/article/download/11759/11386> (r4, confirmed, scholarly/critical) — Open-access review, read in full. Praises the book but warns that some links rest on resemblance, not documented influence. Crossref dates the DOI 2021; the issue is December 2011. A Polish version is in Przeglad Socjologii Jakosciowej 7(3), 2011, pp. 144-153.
- **[@lanier1930]** Lanier, L. H. *Review of Progressive Relaxation (Jacobson 1929)*. American Journal of Psychology 42(3):473-475. 1930. <https://doi.org/10.2307/1415459> (r3, read, scholarly) — Contemporary review, cited by Nathoo 2016 (on 'neuro-muscular hypertension' vs 'neurasthenia'). JSTOR blocked; not read. Other contemporary reviews found via Crossref, none read: NEJM 1929 (10.1056/nejm192906272002623), JAMA 1929 (10.1001/jama.1929.02710100055041), Annals of Internal Medicine 1930 (10.7326/0003-4819-3-11-1167_1), Archives of Neurology and Psychiatry 1930 (10.1001/archneurpsyc.1930.02220180197020), and on the 2nd ed.: Pattie, Am J Psychol 1939 (10.2307/1416693).
- **[@klepinger2021]** Laurah E. Klepinger. *Post-Lineage Yoga: From Guru to #MeToo [review]*. Race and Yoga 6.1 (UC Berkeley, eScholarship). 2021. <https://escholarship.org/uc/item/67j0w928> (r4, confirmed, critical) — Read in full. Critiques the book's limited treatment of race, privilege and appropriation. No yoga nidra mention.
- **[@leroy2019]** Le Roy, J. *Yoga-nidra par Pierre Bonnasse*. Eveil et philosophie (blog, Canalblog). 2019. <https://eveilphilosophie.canalblog.com/archives/2019/06/12/37424368.html> (r2, confirmed, community/practitioner, fr) — 12 June 2019. Announcement by the author's friend (a Douglas Harding-line blogger) plus a ~600-word passage by Bonnasse, apparently from the book's opening pages. The passage calls the 108 practices 'le Rosaire du cœur' and rejects effortful wakefulness during sleep. Not an independent review.
- **[@mattison2022]** Mattison, C. *Rest Is Resistance (starred review)*. BookPage. 2022. <https://www.bookpage.com/reviews/rest-is-resistance-tricia-hersey-book-review/> (r3, confirmed, critical) — October 2022, starred. Stresses Hersey's denunciation of the commodified wellness industry ('shallow wellness work'). Practices named: prayer, daydreams, sleep, intense laughter. Notes that the audiobook is read by the author.
- **[@sharp2012]** Michelle Sharp. *Review of Yoga Skills for Therapists (Yoga International, Fall 2012, p. 90)*. 2012. <https://yogafordepression.com/wp-content/uploads/yoga-skills-yoga-international-review.pdf> (r2, confirmed)
- **[@musto2023]** Musto, S. & Hazard Vallerand, A. *Exploring the uses of yoga nidra: An integrative review*. Journal of Nursing Scholarship. 2023. <https://doi.org/10.1111/jnu.12927> (r4, read, scholarly, cited ×1) — Nursing-science integrative review; via compiler discover (Europe PMC) [read: abstract only]
- **[@munoz2018]** Muñoz, A. *James Mallinson y Mark Singleton (trad., ed. e introd.), Roots of Yoga, Londres, Penguin Books, 2017, 540 pp.* Estudios de Asia y África 53(1). 2018. (r4, *unverified*, scholarly, es) — Indologist's review, known only via Wikipedia: chapter introductions are technical, vernacular sources (Braj Bhāṣā) are light, translations are reliable. Not reached: the SciELO URL could not be resolved.
- **[@newcombe2006]** Newcombe, S. *Elizabeth De Michelis, A History of Modern Yoga: Patanjali and Western Esotericism (review)*. Asian Medicine 2(1):89-91 (Brill). 2006. <https://doi.org/10.1163/15734218-00201009> (r4, confirmed, scholarly/historical) — Metadata confirmed via Crossref; Unpaywall lists it as open access, but Brill returned 403. Not read.
- **[@noe2014]** Noë, A. *In Search Of A Science Of Consciousness*. NPR, 13.7: Cosmos and Culture (30 Dec 2014). 2014. <https://www.npr.org/sections/13.7/2014/12/30/373952810/in-search-of-a-science-of-consciousness> (r3, confirmed, popular) — Short appreciative piece by a philosopher: meditators as collaborators in a science of experience; the Indian taxonomy of states. Nothing specific on yoga nidra.
- **[@nugteren2008]** Nugteren, A. *Book Reviews (A History of Modern Yoga)*. Aries 8(1):107-109 (Brill). 2008. <https://doi.org/10.1163/157005908X246751> (r4, confirmed, scholarly) — Crossref gives 2008; Wikipedia gives 2007. Not read; Wikipedia's paraphrase: a 'preliminary overview' with a workable typology.
- **[@ogilvie2001]** Ogilvie, R.D. *The process of falling asleep*. Sleep Medicine Reviews 5(3):247-270. 2001. <https://doi.org/10.1053/smrv.2001.0145> (r5, read, scholarly, cited ×1) — Standard review of sleep onset (N1, hypnagogia); not about yoga nidra [read: abstract only]
- **[@pandiperumal2022]** Pandi-Perumal, S.R. et al. *The Origin and Clinical Relevance of Yoga Nidra*. Sleep and Vigilance 6(1):61-84. 2022. <https://doi.org/10.1007/s41782-022-00202-7> (r3, read, scholarly, cited ×1)
- **[@pflueger2011]** Pflueger, L. W. *Yoga Body: The Origins of Modern Posture Practice - By Mark Singleton*. Religious Studies Review 37(3): 235 (DOI 10.1111/j.1748-0922.2011.01543_4.x). 2011. <https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1111/j.1748-0922.2011.01543_4.x> (r4, read, scholarly) — Crossref metadata only; paywalled; not read.
- **[@weekly2022]** Publishers Weekly. *Rest Is Resistance: A Manifesto (review)*. Publishers Weekly. 2022. <https://www.publishersweekly.com/978-0-316-36521-5> (r3, confirmed, critical) — Reviewed 12 Aug 2022. Calls it an empathetic, sermon-like treatise. Critique: the author 'sometimes struggles to find new ways to expand on her central point'. Gives family details (father died at 55; grandmother's half-hour naps). Lists 224 pp. and a CD audio ISBN.
- **[@reed2017]** Reed, H. *Review of Yoga Nidra Meditation: The Sleep of the Sages*. Intuitive Connections Network. 2017. <http://intuitive-connections.net/2017/book-yoga-nidra.html> (r2, confirmed, community) — Brief, warm review by Henry Reed (dream researcher, Edgar Cayce milieu); followed by the publisher's excerpt.
- **[@remski2017]** Remski, M. *10 Things We Didn't Know About Yoga Until This New Must-Read Dropped*. Yoga Journal. 2017. <https://www.yogajournal.com/yoga-101/10-things-didnt-know-yoga-history> (r3, confirmed, practitioner) — Popular review. Quotes the book on vinyāsa meaning the installation of mantras on the body (nyāsa) in tantric texts, and on the yogic body being 'written' by tradition rather than observed. Useful for the nyāsa and body-rotation discussion.
- **[@reviewschultz1932]** *Review of Schultz, Das autogene Training (Zeitschrift für Psychologie, 1932)*. 1932. <https://www.google.com/books/feeds/volumes/hgGLBEzo4t8C> (r2, read, de) — Snippets: 'passive Sitz-, Droschkenkutscher- oder Liegehaltung'; Übungsdauer extended to 5-15 minutes, later up to half an hour.
- **[@ridder2015]** Ridder, A. *The iRest Program for Healing PTSD (book review)*. LA Yoga Magazine. 2015. <https://layoga.com/entertainment/books-dvds/irest-program-healing-ptsd/> (r2, confirmed, popular) — Short review dated 2 March 2015. Reports chapters 4-12 as 'The Ten Tools of iRest'; quotes Miller's definition of bodysensing and his universal 'iRest can help heal' promise. The reviewer is a US Army public affairs specialist.
- **[@scott1992]** Scott, D.F. *Review of Hypnagogia: The Unique State of Consciousness Between Wakefulness and Sleep, by Andreas Mavromatis (Routledge 1991)*. British Journal of Psychiatry 160(1):147. 1992. <https://doi.org/10.1192/s0007125000035480> (r3, confirmed, scholarly) — Reviewer from Clinical Neurophysiology Dept, Royal London Trust. Describes three parts; Part One (phenomena) most interesting; loosening-of-ego-boundaries thesis persuasive rather than totally convincing; recommends the book.
- **[@sims2017]** Sims, N. *Book Review of Roots of Yoga, Translated and Edited by James Mallinson and Mark Singleton*. The Indian Philosophy Blog. 2017. <https://indianphilosophyblog.org/2017/12/30/book-review-of-roots-of-yoga-translated-and-edited-by-james-mallinson-and-mark-singleton-reviewed-by-neil-sims/> (r4, confirmed, scholarly) — Academic review (Univ. of New Mexico). Quotes the book on not being a manual of practice (p. xii); notes the 58-footnote introduction, the timeline, and the inclusion of Yogatārāvalī and Amṛtasiddhi passages; finds the editors non-partisan.

Psychiatrisch-neurologische Wochenschrift. 1932. <https://books.google.com/books?id=S9UOO8AqD_QC> (r3, read, historical, de) — One Google Books snippet only. A contemporary review that singles out the yoga chapter ('S. 257 bis 268') and the formula-like resolutions.
- **[@unknown2015]** unknown. *Review of Yoga Nidra for Complete Relaxation and Stress Relief*. Metapsychology Online Reviews. c. 2015-2016. <https://metapsychology.net/?p=7558> (r3, *unverified*, critical) — The only long-form review found; the site could not be reached (DNS failure / egress proxy). Read it if possible.
- **[@variouseg2012]** various (e.g., Becker, K. J. 2016). *Amazon.de customer reviews of Ramm-Bonwitt, Yoga-Nidra: Der Schlaf der Yogis (Schirner eds.)*. Amazon.de. 2012-2025. <https://www.amazon.de/dp/3897673002> (r2, read, community, de) — 14 ratings, average 4.9/5, as of Oct 2026. Main critiques: only about 30 pp. of practice and a misleading subtitle (Becker 2016); negative phrasing and fast pace on the CD (2012). The 1984 Bauer edition has 2 ratings, 5/5.
- **[@walsh2011]** Walsh, M. *Asanas on ice: A History of Modern Yoga (book review)*. Elephant Journal. 2011. <https://www.elephantjournal.com/2011/10/234462/> (r2, confirmed, practitioner/popular) — Long-form review by a yoga teacher, read in full. It is the only source found that describes the Conclusion's rite-of-passage reading of the class: separation = centering, transition = asanas, incorporation = savasana. It calls the Conclusion 'a few pages' and the book 'dry' but meticulously researched.
- **[@weintraub2015]** Weintraub, A. *Review of The iRest Program for Healing PTSD (IAYT media review)*. International Association of Yoga Therapists. 2015. <https://cdn.ymaws.com/www.iayt.org/resource/resmgr/docs_yttmediareviews/weintraub_review_of_r_miller.pdf> (r3, confirmed, practitioner) — Read in full (1 p.). The most detailed description of the book's structure: 41 practice sessions, PTSD self-test, research chapter, 'ten tools', 'little and often', writing exercises, no kosha language, readers may record and reword the scripts. The reviewer is a LifeForce Yoga founder and is not independent of the field; repeats a '4500 years' antiquity claim. Year approximate.
- **[@williamson2010]** Williamson, L. *A History of Modern Yoga: Patañjali and Western Esotericism by Elizabeth De Michelis*. Alternative Spirituality and Religion Review 1(2):149-152. 2010. <https://doi.org/10.5840/asrr2010126> (r4, confirmed, scholarly) — Not read; summarised on Wikipedia as 'a comprehensive overview'.

## Theses

- **[@garnierpetit2017]** Garnier-Petit, M. *La préparation à la naissance et à la parentalité sophrologique à travers le vécu des femmes : étude qualitative phénoménologique dans une maternité de type III*. Université Clermont Auvergne (midwifery dissertation), DUMAS. 2017. <https://dumas.ccsd.cnrs.fr/dumas-01828964/document> (r2, confirmed, practitioner, fr) — Gives a full example basic sophronisation script (head to feet, longer out-breath, 'at the very edge of sleep', 2-min integration pause, return), citing Raoul 2010, and the terpnos logos definition citing Chéné, Initiation à la sophrologie (12th ed., pp. 22-25). Women describe recalling the guide's voice during labour.
- **[@klipfel2011]** Klipfel, B. *La sophrologie en odontologie : enquête d'opinion, intérêt et possibilités d'applications*. Université Henri Poincaré – Nancy 1 (dental surgery dissertation). 2011. <https://hal.univ-lorraine.fr/hal-01738920/document> (r2, confirmed, practitioner, fr) — Summarises Chéné, Sophrologie tome 1 (4th ed. 2001 and 5th ed. 2008): the sources (yoga to RDC1, Buddhism, Zen to RDC3), levels and states of consciousness, the Isocay systems, postures, and a modelled script of the basic sophronisation (pp. 106-108 of the dissertation). Student work, sympathetic to sophrology.
- **[@marquilly2019]** Marquilly, L. *Apports de la sophrologie en orthophonie : revue de la littérature*. Université de Picardie Jules Verne (speech therapy dissertation), DUMAS. 2019. <https://dumas.ccsd.cnrs.fr/dumas-02980227/document> (r2, confirmed, practitioner, fr) — Names Raja yoga, Tibetan Buddhist meditation and Japanese Zen, 'stripped of their religious aspects', as Caycedo's Eastern sources (citing Fiorletta 2010). Cites Chéné 2008 tome 1.
- **[@maruejouls2008]** Maruéjouls, A. *Utilisation de la sophrologie par les médecins généralistes : une étude exploratoire*. Université de Grenoble (medical thesis), DUMAS. 2008. <https://dumas.ccsd.cnrs.fr/dumas-00638461/document> (r2, confirmed, practitioner, fr) — History (journey to Asia 1965-67), the sophroliminal level, the terpnos logos (citing Audouin 2004), the structure of a sophronisation. Cites Étévenon & Santerre 2006 on EEG. Background only.
- **[@mavromatis1983]** Mavromatis, A. *Hypnagogia: The Nature and Function of the Hypnagogic State*. Brunel University (PhD thesis, Department of Psychology; supervisor J. Richardson). 1983. <https://bura.brunel.ac.uk/handle/2438/6638> (r4, confirmed, scholarly) — Free full text on the university repository (2 PDF vols, 581 pp.), the text the 1987 book was adapted from. Read in relevant parts. Contains no mention of yoga nidra or Satyananda. Useful pages (printed): methods and induction techniques 19-24; sounds, smells, myoclonic jerks and falling 51-57; definition and stages pegged to relaxation 73-82; hypnosis ch. 8 pp. 139-149; meditation ch. 10 pp. 183-211 (Patanjali's first five limbs as deliberate hypnagogia, p. 184); 'vrittis' claim via D.M. Baker 1980, p. 397; tenth-century Tantric 'junction of the states / half-sleep / Fourth State' quoted third-hand (Esnoul via de Becker 1968), p. 485; Green et al. 1971a Menninger yogi aware during 4 cps EEG, p. 497; 'physiologically asleep, psychologically awake' hypothesis, p. 504.
- **[@moszeik2016]** Moszeik, E. N. *Die Wirkung einer Kurzform von Yoga Nidra Meditation auf Stresserleben, Schlafqualität und Wohlbefinden. Eine experimentelle Evaluationsstudie*. FernUniversität in Hagen (repository ub-deposit). 2016. <https://ub-deposit.fernuni-hagen.de/receive/mir_mods_00000775> (r3, *unverified*, scholarly, de) — Earlier thesis where the 11-minute Satyananda-based short form was developed; basis of Moszeik et al. 2020. Known only from the dissertation's citation and quote (p. 90); the repository page was blocked by a security check.
- **[@moszeik2023]** Moszeik, E.N. *Die Wirkung von Yoga Nidra Meditation auf emotionale, kognitive und biologische Faktoren des Wohlbefindens*. Dissertation, Universität der Bundeswehr München; also Springer Fachmedien Wiesbaden 2026 (doi 10.1007/978-3-658-5032…). 2023. <https://athene-forschung.unibw.de/doc/144964/144964.pdf> (r4, read, scholarly/cultural, de, cited ×6) — Read German abstract and sections via PDF. n=362 online RCT: 11 vs 30 min Yoga Nidra vs music (active control) vs waitlist; effects very small (d=0.08-0.16); vs music only depression (d=0.13); cortisol changes only when practice frequency is taken into account. Contrasts YN with hypnosis (self- vs other-suggestion)
- **[@rusu2012]** Rusu, L. R. *Autorégulation et dépendance : intervention de sevrage tabagique par la sophrologie et les TCC*. Université de Lorraine / Université Paris Descartes (PhD thesis). 2012. <https://hal.univ-lorraine.fr/tel-01749425/document> (r3, confirmed, scholarly, fr) — Defines the basic sophronisation and the sophroliminal level, and the terpnos logos (calm, monotone voice), citing Chéné, La Sophrologie (Ellébore). Smoking-cessation trial combining sophrology and CBT. Background only.
- **[@sriharisharma2021]** Srihari Sharma K. N.; Subramanya, Pailoor; Bhat, Prabhavathi. *Effect of integrated approach of yoga therapy based rehabilitation program on left ventricular dysfunction secondary to coronary artery disease - A randomized control trial (Appendix 1: IAYT module incl. full DRT script)*. SVYASA. 2021. <https://elibraryofyoga.com/handle/123456789/1871> (r3, confirmed) — Appendix PDF https://elibraryofyoga.com/bitstreams/3897c472-3118-4649-85d6-dca89ceff2f4/download gives the 8-phase DRT: toes-to-head relaxation with A/U/M chanting, then body seen 'collapsed', blue-sky expansion, return with Om. No sankalpa. Also gives the MSRT script.
- **[@wildcroft2019]** Theodora Wildcroft. *Patterns of authority and practice relationships in 'post-lineage yoga'*. The Open University (PhD, Religious Studies). 2019. <https://oro.open.ac.uk/59125/1/thesis_TRW19.pdf> (r4, *unverified*) — Free on the OU repository, but blocked from here (403). Precursor to the 2020 book; the cheapest way to check its yoga nidra content.

## Primary texts

- **[@bhattacharyayt]** Bhattacharya, R.S. (ed., comm.). *Yoga Taravali (Śaṅkarācārya), with Hindi commentary by Ram Shankar Bhattacharya*. <https://archive.org/details/yoga-taravali-shankracharya-ram-shankar-bhattacharya-c> (r3, confirmed, historical/cultural, hi, cited ×4) — Yogatārāvalī is one of the texts where yoganidrā names a samādhi-like state (Birch 2015). Archive.org record (Hindi, undated scan)
- **[@faliero1998]** Faliero, M. (data entry). *Vijñānabhairava (Sanskrit e-text)*. GRETIL, SUB Göttingen. 1998 (TEI 2020). <https://gretil.sub.uni-goettingen.de/gretil/corpustei/transformations/plaintext/sa_vijJAnabhairava.txt> (r4, confirmed, historical, sa) — Free Sanskrit text (CC BY-NC-SA). Verse 75 is the dhāraṇā at the threshold of sleep (anāgatāyāṃ nidrāyām...). Verse 82 is contemplating the body as unsupported while seated or lying. Numbering follows the GRETIL source.
- **[@nikhilananda1936]** Nikhilananda, Swami (tr.). *The Mandukya Upanishad with Gaudapada's Karika and Sankara's Commentary*. Sri Ramakrishna Ashrama, Mysore. 1936. <https://archive.org/details/mandukya-upanishad-karika-nikhilananda-rkm> (r4, confirmed, historical/practitioner, cited ×2) — The four states incl. turīya; year from memory (first ed. 1936), archive record undated
- **[@pargiter1904]** Pargiter, F.E. (tr.). *The Markandeya Purana (translated by F. Eden Pargiter)*. Asiatic Society, Bibliotheca Indica, Calcutta. 1904. <https://archive.org/details/in.ernet.dli.2015.196011> (r4, read, historical, public domain, cited ×3) — Read canto 81 (= Devī Māhātmya ch. 1) in the archive.org full text: Pargiter renders yoganidrā as 'the sleep of contemplation' / 'contemplation-sleep' and has Brahmā praise 'Vishnu's Sleep, which is Queen of the universe'
- **[@sauton1952]** Sauton, M. *Mandukya Upanishad (traduction française)*. 1952. <https://archive.org/details/mandukya-upanishad-sauton> (r3, confirmed, historical/cultural, fr, cited ×4) — French translation; shows the Māṇḍūkya's reception in the French milieu that also produced Boyes 1973. Confirmed via archive.org record
- **[@sinh1914]** Sinh, P. (tr.). *The Hatha Yoga Pradipika (translated by Pancham Sinh)*. Panini Office, Allahabad. 1914. <https://archive.org/details/hathayogapradipi0000panc> (r3, confirmed, historical, public domain, cited ×2) — Old, free translation; use a modern critical translation for verse numbers (Birch 2015; Mallinson & Singleton 2017). Confirmed via archive.org record
- **[@stebbins1892]** Stebbins, G. *Dynamic Breathing and Harmonic Gymnastics*. Edgar S. Werner, New York. 1892. <https://archive.org/details/DynamicBreathingAndHarmonicGymnastics> (r3, confirmed, historical, cited ×1) — Public domain; relevant chapters read. Exercise IV 'Yoga Breathing' (p. 86): lying relaxed, the breath is imagined through a sequence of body parts (feet, hands, knees, elbows, hips, shoulders, abdomen, solar plexus, chest, head). Ch. X 'Relaxation Exercises' (p. 91 ff.) includes 'Perfect Relaxation': sense yourself as separate from the body, with dreamy imagery. Discussed in Yoga Body ch. 7 (the section title names her). A striking parallel to rotation of consciousness and witness awareness 80 years before Satyananda. Parallel only; no documented link to Satyananda.

Amazon. <https://www.amazon.com/dp/B0CP9S19JT> (r2, read) — Page metadata carries ISBN-13 9798985626643, the same ISBN Amazon shows for the Kindle edition of The Heart of Yoga Nidra.

- **[@lusk2015a]** Lusk, J. T. *Yoga Nidra FAQ on Setting Intentions & Sankalpas*. Wholesome Resources (author's site). n.d. Sankalpa method: a quality rather than a behaviour, arising intuitively, present tense, the 'more and more' softener, repeated near the end 'in delta'. Also repeats the '1 hour = 4 hours sleep' claim.
- **[@lusk]** Lusk, J. T. *Yoga Nidra and Sleep*. Wholesome Resources (author's site). n.d.. <https://wholesomeresources.com/yoga-nidra-sleep/> (r2, confirmed, practitioner) — Separates bedtime use as a sleep aid from the yoga nidra practice itself (warns the habit leads to sleep). Claims 1 hour = 4 hours of sleep, citing 'Saraswati 1998, Kumar 2008', and aware delta. Useful for debate 3 (is falling asleep failure?) and for myth 6.3.
- **[@lusk2016]** Lusk, J. T. *Yoga Nidra's Benefits*. Wholesome Resources (author's site). 2016. <https://wholesomeresources.com/4288/yoga-nidras-benefits/> (r3, confirmed, practitioner) — Reproduces the benefits list from lusk2015 pp. 42-43 with its references (Amita 2009; Rani 2011; Kumar 2007, 2013; Pandya & Kumar 2007; Panda 2003). - **[@lusk2019]** Lusk, J. T. *Yoga Nidra and Your Brain Wave Fluctuations*. Wholesome Resources (author's site). 2019. <https://wholesomeresources.com/6044/yoga-nidra-and-your-brain-wave-fluctuations/> (r1, confirmed, popular) — Lusk's four-stage brainwave account (physical relaxation, abdominal breathing, mental and emotional balance, intuition/theta; aware delta). Claims about delta, telomeres, stem cells and beta-amyloid are taken from Dawson Church's Mind to Matter. A clear example of brainwave pop-science to treat critically.

Goodreads. <https://www.goodreads.com/book/show/19001379> (r2, read, community) — About 4.0 average from about 394 ratings and 38 reviews. Read via an automated page summary.
- **[@yoganidra]** *Yoga nidra (Wikipedia article, raw wikitext)*. Wikipedia. <https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Yoga_nidra&action=raw> (r2, read) — Used to check the stage count. - **[@wikiyoganidra]** *Yoga nidra (Wikipedia)*. <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoga_nidra> (r2, read, popular/community, cited ×3) — Useful snowball: cites Birch & Hargreaves, Singleton 2005, Shearer 2020, Boyes 1973

Yoga Nidra Network. <https://www.yoganidranetwork.org/operating-ethics-2/> (r3, confirmed, official) — States that Total Yoga Nidra was developed as a response to exclusionary and harmful experiences in traditional yoga nidra lineages. Post-lineage, anti-racist, queer-affirming, trauma-informed commitments. Useful for debate 6 in 08-debates.
- **[@networka]** Yoga Nidra Network. *Academic Resources (Yoga Nidra Network)*. Yoga Nidra Network. <https://www.yoganidranetwork.org/academic-resources/> (r2, confirmed, official) — The Network's reading list: Parker et al. 2013, Birch & Hargreaves 2015 (set reading for their training), Kjaer 2002 (repeats the '65% dopamine' figure uncritically), Kumar studies, and McGonigal on sankalpa (Yoga International 2010-11). Quotes Satyananda's 1964 convention talk (YOGA vol. 3 no. 2, 1965) on 'revising' an old tantric practice. Shows how the Network handles evidence.

YogaUOnline. 2015. <https://yogauonline.com/?p=14065> (r2, read, popular) — Unsigned profile (9 Apr 2015): five years of Viniyoga study in India; met Jean Klein in 1984, which led to the non-dual reframing and to iRest.
